Some of the products that come to our country from these countries contain chemicals that pose a danger to human health. These products are found in households and workplaces, food fed to babies, toys used by children, and stationery materials, such as diet pills and cosmetic products. The vast majority of our people continue to use Chinese and Far East goods because they are cheap, not knowing the damage they do to human health. Public institutions do not have enough audits on this issue, and there is no healthy public information. Some products are considered to be a problem for the country's economy, as well as a danger for public health. The construction industry, including the textile industry, the dye and chemical industry, the chemical industry, is negatively affected by all sectors and domestic manufacturers, imports and illegal goods from China and the Far East countries, including the hardware industry, toy industry and toy industry. Despite their cheapness, these products cause problems for our consumers in the medium and long term due to their lack of quality and intensity. With the value and employment opportunities that it creates, our textile and confectionary sector is the most affected sector by Chinese and Far East-based products. Unfair competition by textiles and confectionary products imported from China has shut down hundreds of businesses in the domestic market. There are some obstacles to all world customs against cheap and unhealthy products from China and the Far East. However, the obstacles in our country remain inadequate. For these reasons, we have reason to believe that it would be appropriate to open a parliamentary study of goods imported from China and the Far East or smuggled into the home, on our consumers in terms of health and quality, and in order to determine the measures that should be taken by the Supreme Assembly, which should be taken by our producers in terms of unfair competition. Article 16 of the Human and Citizen Rights Declaration of 1789 states that the separation of forces is a necessary factor for the constitutional state. Clearly, political structures that do not apply the principle of separation of forces cannot have a constitution. The principle of separation of forces is one of the main elements that makes a country's political system democratic because only an independent judiciary can implement the rule of law. So the principle of separation is, in a sense, a precondition for the rule of law, which makes it possible for the judiciary to be separate from the law and the administration. tbmm-2014-01-22sit04spe0324par0216	Members of parliament, the structure of the Supreme Council of Judges and Prosecutors in the 2010 Constitutional Amendment was based on pluralism. However, the Constitutional Court's decision by pluralism in the Council to cancel the Matuf arrangements has led to a majority structure of the Council. Basically, the offer we're negotiating is to address the negatives of the majority structure and the internal interference in the judiciary. Compared to the fact that the judiciary has a peaceful function, the judiciary is now the most important item of Turkey's agenda, which is based on the explanation of how much the proposed proposal is needed. Congressmans, the principle of division of forces does not mean that there will be no link between legislation, executive and judicial bodies. The laws passed by law in democracies bind governance and judgment. Again, the process of executive and legislative action is also subject to judicial supervision. Laws also depend on the judiciary in terms of compliance with the Constitution. So, when the law and the administration violates the field of duty that has been drawn for them, judicial control will step in, the authority to make final decisions will belong to the judicial body. What, though, if the law itself exceeds its constitutional jurisdiction or invokes illegal authority? Who's going to control that he's in violation of judicial independence? This brings to mind a statement made by the Supreme Council of Judges and Prosecutors a while ago. In fact, the Supreme Council of Judges and Prosecutors has established a view that would put pressure on the court regarding a regulatory procedure in which it has appointed its members. This is clearly a constitutional violation of judicial independence, and there is no application mechanism for the Supreme Council of Judges and Prosecutors to violate the Constitution. In this case, the balance between legislation, executive and judicial bodies has been corrupted in favour of the judiciary. However, it is also imperative that the legislative body, which uses the fundamental authority based on sovereignty, arranges within the borders of the Constitution. As a matter of fact, the fourth paragraph of the Constitution's initial provisions states that the division of forces does not mean the order of superiority among state bodies, but that there is a civilized work and cooperation among men, and that the rule is only in the Constitution and the law. Members of parliament, the proposed amendment, in light of these assessments, is in violation of the principles of division of forces and judicial independence, there is no doubt that these principles will serve to gain rationality. The second important issue, dear friends, is the fiscal independence of the judiciary. tbmm-2014-01-22sit06spe0647par0110	Erol Dora, Mardin MP who wants a word on the motion.
tbmm-2014-01-22sit06spe0650par0112	I have made a commitment to the motion we have made on the 4th Amendment. I salute the General Assembly. When we look at our Constitution, it is said that according to Article 2 of our Constitution, the Republic of Turkey is a democratic, secular and social state. Article 138 set up the independence of the judiciary. In Article 159, the HSYK states that the independence of the courts and the assurance of the judiciary are established and served on its basis. Of course, according to our Constitution, Turkey is a democratic, secular legal state, but when we look at our practice today, one of the things that is essential to a democratic country is the separation of forces, the existence of the system of powers. So, the law, the administration and the judiciary must have been completely separated and they must not have ruled over each other. We, the Peace and Democracy Party, offered our proposal to the Constitutional Compromise Commission on the structure of HSYK. In this proposal, we argued that the HSYK would be replaced by the existing Constitution and that the autonomy of this council would be guaranteed constitutionally. As a party, we view the structure of HSYK as one of the basis for the principle of separation of forces. Therefore, it is vital that the council be neutral and independent, that it be able to make decisions apart from the political authorities, that it has an autonomy budget and that it is fully autonomous. Again, in accordance with the independent structure of the Council, appointment and disciplinary procedures must be carried out independently of political authorities. The breaking of the current structure of the council is also an important parameter in transforming it into a democratic structure. In this context, we consider it an essential condition for members of the Council to be elected democratically. In this context, the diversity of resources selected for the high judicial council in the election should be based on the criteria for democratic selection in the sources where elected members come from. Again, the fact that the Ministry of Justice has influence and authority over the Council, as it is based on the Venice Criteria, no doubt, will lead to the independence of the Council. In addition to legislation and execution, the judge, who performs an entirely different task, should be kept out of all pressure and influence, so the Justice Minister's presence in the Council should be symbolic. On the contrary, the slightest interference of the execution will cause the legality of judicial decisions to be questioned and the politicalisation of the judiciary. After reviewing the proposal text, the position of the Minister of Justice, the chairman of the HSYK, is clearly arranged to undermine the independence of the judiciary. In this way, it is also contrary to the Copenhagen Criterias, when the Minister of Justice, a member of the executive body, has increased its activity on HSYK, which has been appointed over its members of the judiciary. As you know, we are now continuing full membership negotiations with the European Union. The most important criteria for our full membership in the European Union are the Copenhagen Criteria. What's all this? Democracy, rule of law, human rights. Now, here, how are we supposed to practice the rule of law? For the rule of law to take place in practice, the separation of forces in particular must have happened in practice. At the same time, when we look at the Council of Europe, the Venice Commission, which is one of the Council of Europe's legal institutions, and at the same time, when we examine the decisions, reports of the Council of European Judges, again, we see that this arrangement is contrary to the separation of forces and the establishment of a democratic country. In that respect, I believe that, first of all, all political parties must join forces, and again, I salute the General Assembly, in order to make a constitutional amendment first, in order for the real division of forces in Turkey to be practically realized.
